Remarks.&mdash;During January the highest reading of the barometer was 30.450, 011 the 22nd; and the lowest 30.100, 011 the 8th and 10th. The highest temperature was 73&deg;, on the nth; the lowest 390, on the 20th; and the mean during month was 54&deg;.2. N.E. winds prevailed, and the strongest was recorded on the 12th, averaging 12.6 miles an hour during 24 hours. Rain fell 011 n days, measuring 0.53 inch. During February the highest reading of the barometer was 30.414, 011 the 22nd; and the lowest 29.810, 011 the 3rd and 4th. The highest temperature was 770, on the 26th; the lowest 450, on the 6th and 7th; and the mean during mouth was 590.1. N.E. winds prevailed, and the strongest was recorded ou the 5th, averaging 13.1 miles an hour during 24 hours. Rain fell on 12 days, measuring 1.57 inches. During March the highest reading of the barometer was 30.458, on the 17th; and the lowest 29.806, on the 13th. The highest temperature was 82&deg;, on the 14th; the lowest 40&deg;, on the 17th; and the mean during month was 62&deg;.4. Easterly winds prevailed; the strongest recorded was from the N.W. on the 16th, averaging 16.8 miles an hour during 24 hours. Rain fell 011 11 days, measuring 1.06 inches. IX. NOTICES TO MARINERS.. HINA SEA. No. 290. HAINAN STRAIT-KIINOCHOW DISTRICT. CAPE CAMI IJGHTHOUSK NoTiE is hereby given that the light at Cape Cami was exhibited forthe Arst time atsset on the ist instant. The iuninating appaxatus is revolving dioptrio of the fourthoorder, showing double wite flashes at M&quot;ntervals of half a minute. The lighthouse stands about 83 yards north of high-water mark on the southernmost point of the islet iarked on the charts.as Cae Cami, and the light, which is elevated 67 feet above the level of the sea, should be visible in clgr weather at a distance of 13r nautical miles in al directions where it is not obscured by land. The tower is an iron screw pile structure, 55 feet high, with a total height from its base to the lantern vane of 671 feet. The tower and dwellings are painted white. Approximate position:-- Latitude.. .. .0.. ..o13&#39;2o&#39;fN. Longitude . . . . . . . . . . . . 1o9&quot; 55S&#39; 5&quot; E. CAUTION. Onaccount of the outlying reefs about Cape Osmi, vessels are recommended not to round it at any less distance than 2 miles from the lighthouse. SHANGHA, 8th Janarty 1895. No. 291. In conformity wth the instructions contained in your Circular NO. 381, Second Series, I now transmit tthe Statistical Department at Shanghai, for publication, the statistics of the trade at Kowloon and the neighbouring atations in Native vessels for the fihat quarter of the year 1895, January-March. b. The Custom House (A )was opened on the 2nd April x887,%nd the collection of Provincial I4 an Ching-tei Tax on General Cargo, at the rates fixed by the Provincial Tariiff received from the Viceroy of the Two Kwang, began on that day. The collection of Tariff Import D4ty and Convention ikhin on Opinm (Rk.ph 3o and k. 8o per picul) commenced on the x4th April 1887, and the collection of Native Duties on General Cargo, at the rs fed by the Native Onstom House Tariff received from the Hoppo, on the ist July 1887. The stations at present ontrollpd by the Kowloon Onstoms are own as the Openimoon ( * ), Changehow ( g ), Fotochow A O), and Kowloon (IL If) stations. W.
